) by changing the area of the small loops
\bar{l}_{IJ,\epsilon}
.
Specifically we assume the existence of semianalytic diffeomorphisms
{\bar{\phi}}_{I,c_{I},\epsilon}
such that the loop
\bar{l}_{IJ,,c_{I},\epsilon}
has a coordinate area which is
c_{I}
times the coordinate
area of its counterpart
\bar{l}_{IJ,\epsilon}
with the loop
\bar{l}_{IJ,,c_{I},\epsilon}
defined as:

\bar{l}_{IJ,c_{I},\epsilon}=({e_{J}}_{(t_{\epsilon,J,I,c_{I}},0)})^{-1}\circ{\bar{\phi}}_{I,c_{I},\epsilon}({e_{J}}_{(t_{\epsilon,J,I,c_{I}},0)})\circ{e_{I}}_{(\epsilon_{I},0)}

(5.49)

Here
{\bar{\phi}}_{I,c_{I},\epsilon}
is subject to similar properties as
{\bar{\phi}}_{I,\epsilon}
i.e.

(i)
{}_{c_{I}}

{\bar{\phi}}_{I,c_{I},\epsilon}
is the identity outside a ball
U_{c_{I},\epsilon}
of radius
{\bf O}(\epsilon)
around
v
.
(ii)
{}_{c_{I}}

{\bar{\phi}}_{I,c_{i},\epsilon}
maps
e_{I}
to itself.

(iii)
{}_{c_{I}}

{\bar{\phi}}_{I,c_{I},\epsilon}
displaces
v
to
v_{I,\epsilon}
where as in section
2.3.1
,
v_{I,\epsilon}
is located at a coordinate distance
\epsilon
from
v
along the edge
e_{I}
, the coordinates
being the RNC at
v
.

{\bar{\phi}}_{I,c_{I},\epsilon}
subject to (i)
{}_{c_{I}}
- (iii)
{}_{c_{I}}
is constructed in Appendix
D.2
.

In equation (
5.49
), the parameter value
t_{J}=t_{\epsilon,J,I,c_{I}}
is the parameter value at which the edge
e_{J}
intersects the boundary of the ball
U_{c_{I},\epsilon}
.
We emphasize that while the area of the small loops change by a factor of
c_{I}
, the position of the vertex
v_{I,\epsilon}
is unchanged.
With this change, we have, to leading order in
\epsilon
, for holonomy evaluations on smooth (i.e.
C^{r}
connections), that:

h_{\bar{l}_{IJ,c_{I},\epsilon}}-{\bf 1}=c_{I}(h_{\bar{l}_{IJ,\epsilon}}-{\bf 1})

(5.50)

so that (
5.34
) takes the form

\int_{0}^{t_{\epsilon,J,I}}dt_{J}{\dot{e}}^{b}_{J}{\hat{e}}^{a}_{I,\epsilon}{F}_{ab}^{i}\tau_{i}=-\frac{(h_{{\bar{l}}_{IJ,\epsilon}}-{\bf 1})}{\epsilon}+O(\epsilon^{2})=-\frac{(h_{{\bar{l}}_{IJ,c_{I}\epsilon}}-{\bf 1})}{c_{I}\epsilon}+O(\epsilon^{2}).

(5.51)

Using (
5.51
), (
5.49
) instead of (
5.34
), (
5.35
) in the analysis of section
5.2
subsequent to (
5.34
) it is straightforward to obtain:

\int_{B_{2\epsilon}(v)}\mathrm{d}^{3}x{\hat{e}}^{a}_{I_{v},\epsilon}{\hat{F}}_{ab}^{k}{\hat{E}}_{k}^{b}(S_{\lambda})_{\lambda}=-i\frac{({\hat{U}}_{{\bar{\phi}}_{I,c_{I},\epsilon}}-{\bf 1})}{c_{I}\epsilon}(S_{\lambda})_{\lambda}.

(5.52)

Using (
5.52
) in (
5.32
), restoring the ‘
v
’ subscripts and denoting the contribution to the operator action
{\hat{O}_{\epsilon}(M,N)}S
from the nondegenerate GR vertices of
S
by

{\hat{O}_{\epsilon}(M,N)}S|_{V_{GR}(S)}

yields:

\displaystyle{\hat{O}_{\epsilon}(M,N)}S|_{V_{GR}(S)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{(\frac{4\pi}{3})^{2}}\sum_{v\in V_{GR}(S)}\sum_{I_{v}=1}^{N_{v}}(\omega_{c}(v){\hat{e}}^{c}_{I}(v))(j_{I_{v}})(j_{I_{v}}+1)\int_{B_{2\epsilon}(v)}\mathrm{d}^{3}x{\hat{e}}^{a}_{I_{v},\epsilon}{\hat{F}}_{ab}^{k}{\hat{E}}_{k}^{b}(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}}

(5.53)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\frac{2}{(\frac{4\pi}{3})^{2}}\sum_{v\in V_{GR}(S)}\sum_{I_{v}=1}^{N_{v}}(\omega_{c}(v){\hat{e}}^{c}_{I}(v)){(j_{I_{v}})(j_{I_{v}}+1)}\frac{({\hat{U}}_{{\bar{\phi}}_{I_{v},c_{I_{v}},\epsilon}}-{\bf 1})}{c_{I_{v}}\epsilon}(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}}.

Finally, we choose

c_{I_{v}}=\frac{128(j_{I_{v}})(j_{I_{v}}+1)}{N_{v}}

(5.54)

to get:

\displaystyle{\hat{O}_{\epsilon}(M,N)}S|_{V_{GR}(S)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{(\frac{4\pi}{3})^{2}}\sum_{v\in V_{GR}(S)}\sum_{I_{v}=1}^{N_{v}}(\omega_{c}(v){\hat{e}}^{c}_{I}(v))(j_{I_{v}})(j_{I_{v}}+1)\int_{B_{2\epsilon}(v)}\mathrm{d}^{3}x{\hat{e}}^{a}_{I_{v},\epsilon}{\hat{F}}_{ab}^{k}{\hat{E}}_{k}^{b}(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}}

(5.55)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i(\frac{3}{8\pi})^{2}\frac{1}{16}\sum_{v\in V_{GR}(S)}\sum_{I_{v}=1}^{N_{v}}(\omega_{c}(v){\hat{e}}^{c}_{I}(v))N_{v}\frac{({\hat{U}}_{{\bar{\phi}}_{I_{v},c_{I_{v}},\epsilon}}-{\bf 1})}{\epsilon}(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}}.

5.2.2
The case when
v
is NGR

In this section we motivate the action of the quantum operator

\int_{B_{2\epsilon}(v)}\mathrm{d}^{3}x{\hat{e}}^{a}_{I_{v},\epsilon}{\hat{F}}_{ab}^{k}{\hat{E}}_{k}^{b}
in (
5.32
).
by thinking of it as a quantization of the classical expression

\int_{B_{2\epsilon}(v)}\mathrm{d}^{3}x{\hat{e}}^{a}_{I_{v},\epsilon}{F}_{ab}^{k}{E}_{k}^{b}
.

Recall that on the Gauss Law constraint surface we have, for any shift vector field
N^{a}
, the identity:

\int_{B_{2\epsilon}(v)}\mathrm{d}^{3}x{N}^{a}{F}_{ab}^{k}{E}_{k}^{b}=\int_{B_{2\epsilon}(v)}\mathrm{d}^{3}x{\cal L}_{\vec{N}}A_{b}^{k}E_{k}^{b}

(5.56)

where
{\cal L}_{\vec{N}}
denotes the Lie derivative with respect to
N^{a}
. At the quantum level, we replace the triad operator by the functional derivative with respect to the connection and
obtain, heuristically, the following operator action on a gauge invariant wave function
\Psi(A)
of the connection

\displaystyle\int_{B_{2\epsilon}(v)}\mathrm{d}^{3}x{N}^{a}{\hat{F}}_{ab}^{k}{\hat{E}}_{k}^{b}\Psi(A)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\int_{B_{2\epsilon}(v)}\mathrm{d}^{3}x{\cal L}_{\vec{N}}A_{b}^{k}\frac{\delta}{\delta A_{b}^{k}}\Psi(A)

(5.57)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\frac{\Psi(A_{b}^{k}+\delta{\cal L}_{\vec{N}}A_{b}^{k})-\Psi(A_{b}^{k})}{\delta}+O(\delta).

We may then replace
A_{b}^{k}+\delta{\cal L}_{\vec{N}}A_{b}^{k}
by
((\phi_{\vec{N},\delta})_{*}A)_{b}^{k}
where
\phi_{\vec{N},\delta}
is a diffeomorphism which translates points an affine amount
\delta

along the orbits of
N^{a}
to obtain a finite
\delta
- approximant
to the operator:

(\int_{B_{2\epsilon}(v)}\mathrm{d}^{3}x{N}^{a}{\hat{F}}_{ab}^{k}{\hat{E}}_{k}^{b})_{\delta}\Psi(A)=-\frac{{\hat{U}}_{\phi_{\vec{N},\delta}}-{\bf 1}}{\delta}\Psi(A)

(5.58)

where the
\delta
subscript indicates that the expression is a finite
\delta
approximant and
{\hat{U}}_{\phi_{\vec{N},\delta}}
is the unitary operator corresponding to the
the diffeomorphism
\phi_{\vec{N},\delta}
.
Setting
N^{a}\equiv{\hat{e}}^{a}_{I_{v},\epsilon}
,
\Psi(A)\equiv(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}}
in the above equation obtains:

(\int_{B_{2\epsilon}(v)}\mathrm{d}^{3}x{\hat{e}}^{a}_{I_{v},\epsilon}{\hat{F}}_{ab}^{k}{\hat{E}}_{k}^{b})_{\delta}(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}}=\frac{{\hat{U}}_{{\bar{\phi}}_{I,\delta}}-{\bf 1}}{\delta}(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}}

(5.59)

where
{\bar{\phi}}_{I_{v},\delta}
is constructed through Appendix
D.2
simply as
{\bar{\phi}}_{I_{v},\delta}\equiv\psi_{\lambda=\delta}
for sufficiently small
\delta
and corresponds exactly to
the desired finite transformation which translates points along the orbits of
{\hat{e}}^{a}_{I_{v},\epsilon}
by an affine amount
\delta
to leading order in
\delta
.

The
\delta\rightarrow 0
limit dual operator action on the off shell basis state
\Psi_{B,f,h}
then defines the
\epsilon
approximant dual operator action:

\displaystyle\Psi_{B,f,h}\left(\int_{B_{2\epsilon}(v)}\mathrm{d}^{3}x{\hat{e}}^{a}_{I_{v},\epsilon}{\hat{F}}_{ab}^{k}{\hat{E}}_{k}^{b}(S_{\lambda})_{\lambda}\right)

(5.60)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\rightarrow 0}\Psi_{B,f,h}\left(\left(\int_{B_{2\epsilon}(v)}\mathrm{d}^{3}x{\hat{e}}^{a}_{I_{v},\epsilon}{\hat{F}}_{ab}^{k}{\hat{E}}_{k}^{b}\right)_{\delta}(S_{\lambda})_{\lambda}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\rightarrow 0}\Psi_{B,f,h}(\frac{({\hat{U}}_{{\bar{\phi}}_{I_{v},\epsilon,\delta}}-{\bf 1})}{\delta}(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}}).

By assumption the
state
(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}}
has an NGR vertex at
v
and, hence, so does its diffeomorphic image by the semianalytic diffeomorphism
{\hat{U}}_{{\bar{\phi}}_{I_{v},\epsilon,\delta}}
.
It follows that the states appearing on the right hand side of (
5.60
) have no overlap with
B
which in turn implies that the right hand side vanishes. Thus for all small enough
\epsilon
we have that:

\Psi_{B,f,h}\left(\int_{B_{2\epsilon}(v)}\mathrm{d}^{3}x{\hat{e}}^{a}_{I_{v},\epsilon}{\hat{F}}_{ab}^{k}{\hat{E}}_{k}^{b}(S_{\lambda})_{\lambda}\right)=0.

(5.61)

5.3
The Continuum Limit Operator
{\hat{O}}(M,N)

In this section we evaluate the continuum limit (dual) operator action on the state
\Psi_{B,f,h}
.
Consider the case that
S
has no overlap with
B
. It follows from (i), section
3.2
that the
embedded colored graph underlying
S
is distinct from the embedded colored graph underlying any
element of
B
. Since
(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}}
lives on the same embedded colored graph as
S
,

(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}}
also cannot have overlap in
B
.
From the discussion around (
3.28
), (
3.29
) in section (
3.4.3
), we have that
any diffeomorphic image of
(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}}
also cannot have overlap in
B
.
Since the actions (
5.55
), (
5.60
) result in states identical to or diffeomorphic to

(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}}
irrespective of whether
v
is GR or NGR, it follows that
the dual operator action amplitude
\Psi_{B,f,h}({\hat{O}}_{\epsilon}(M,N)S)
vanishes for
S
with no overlap
in
B
.

Hence, we need consider amplitudes only for
S
with overlap in
B
. Accordingly let
S
have overlap with
B
.
Two succesive actions of
{\hat{\lambda}}_{v}
on
S
yields
(S_{\lambda})_{\lambda}
which, from an analysis
similar to that underlying (
3.16
) and (
3.19
) indicates that
(S_{\lambda})_{\lambda}
has overlap
in
B
. The discussion around (
3.28
) and (
3.29
) then implies that all diffeomorphic
images of
(S_{\lambda})_{\lambda}
have overlap in
B
.
It then follows from (
5.55
), (
5.54
) that:

\Psi_{B,f,h}({\hat{O}_{\epsilon}(M,N)}S)=-i(\frac{3}{8\pi})^{2}\frac{1}{16}\sum_{v\in V_{GR}(S)}\sum_{I_{v}=1}^{N_{v}}(\omega_{c}(v){\hat{e}}^{c}_{I_{v}}(v))N_{v}\Psi_{B,f,h}(\frac{({\hat{U}}_{{\bar{\phi}}_{I_{v},c_{I_{v}},\epsilon}}-{\bf 1})}{\epsilon}(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}}).

(5.62)

Denoting

{\hat{U}}_{{\bar{\phi}}_{I_{v},c_{I_{v}},\epsilon}}(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}}:=((S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}})_{{\rm diff},I_{v},\epsilon}

(5.63)

we have that

\Psi_{B,f,h}({\hat{U}}_{{\bar{\phi}}_{I_{v},c_{I_{v}},\epsilon}}(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}})=(\prod_{\bar{v}\in V(((S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}})_{{\rm diff},I_{v},\epsilon})}f(\bar{v}))g_{((S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}})_{{\rm diff},I_{v},\epsilon},h}\gamma_{((S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}})_{{\rm diff},I_{v},\epsilon}}.

(5.64)

Diffeomorphism invariance of the RS Volume operator together with (ii),
6
of section
3.1
implies that the gamma factor is invariant under diffeomorphisms (more specifically, see (
3.29
)) so that

\gamma_{((S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}})_{{\rm diff},I_{v},\epsilon}}=\gamma_{(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}}}.

(5.65)

From the fact that no kinks are created by the action of
{\bar{\phi}}_{I_{v},c_{I_{v}},\epsilon}
and the fact that
{\bar{\phi}}_{I_{v},c_{I_{v}},\epsilon}
is non-trivial only it an
\epsilon
-vicinity of
v
, it follows that for small enough
\epsilon
,
we have that:

g_{((S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}})_{{\rm diff},I_{v},\epsilon},h}=g_{S,h}.

(5.66)

Using (
5.65
) and (
5.66
) in (
5.64
), together with the fact that
v
is displaced to
v_{I_{v},\epsilon}
in
((S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}})_{{\rm diff},I_{v},\epsilon}

we obtain

\Psi_{B,f,h}({\hat{U}}_{{\bar{\phi}}_{I_{v},c_{I_{v}},\epsilon}}(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}})=g_{S,h}\gamma_{(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}}}(\prod_{\bar{v}\in V(S),\bar{v}\neq v}f(\bar{v}))f(v_{I_{v},\epsilon}).

(5.67)

Using (
5.67
) in (
5.62
) yields:

\displaystyle\Psi_{B,f,h}({\hat{O}_{\epsilon}(M,N)}S)

(5.68)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i(\frac{3}{8\pi})^{2}\frac{1}{16}g_{S,h}\sum_{v\in V_{GR}(S)}\gamma_{(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}}}\sum_{I_{v}=1}^{N_{v}}(\omega_{c}(v){\hat{e}}^{c}_{I_{v}}(v))(\prod_{\bar{v}\in V(S),\bar{v}\neq v}f(\bar{v}))N_{v}\frac{f(v_{I_{v},\epsilon})-f(v)}{\epsilon}.

Using (
4.35
) in the above equation yields the continuum limit operator amplitude:

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\Psi_{B,f,h}({\hat{O}_{\epsilon}(M,N)}S)

(5.69)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i(\frac{3}{8\pi})^{2}\frac{1}{16}g_{S,h}\sum_{v\in V_{GR}(S)}\gamma_{(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}}}\sum_{I_{v}=1}^{N_{v}}(\omega_{c}(v){\hat{e}}^{c}_{I_{v}}(v))(\prod_{\bar{v}\in V(S),\bar{v}\neq v}f(\bar{v}))N_{v}{\hat{e}}_{I_{v}}^{a}\partial_{a}f(v).

Thus we have that:

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\Psi_{B,f,h}({\hat{O}_{\epsilon}(M,N)}S):=\Psi_{B,f,h}({\hat{O}(M,N)}S)

(5.70)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\;{\rm if}\;S\;{\rm has}\;{\rm no}\;{\rm overlap}\;{\rm in}\;B

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i(\frac{3}{8\pi})^{2}\frac{1}{16}g_{S,h}\sum_{v\in V_{GR}(S)}\gamma_{(S_{\lambda_{v}})_{\lambda_{v}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{I_{v}=1}^{N_{v}}(\omega_{c}(v){\hat{e}}^{c}_{I_{v}}(v))(\prod_{\bar{v}\in V(S),\bar{v}\neq v}f(\bar{v}))N_{v}{\hat{e}}_{I}^{a}\partial_{a}f(v)\;\;{\rm if}\;S\;{\rm has}\;{\rm overlap}\;{\rm in}\;B.

(5.71)

Comparing (
4.71
), (
4.72
) with (
5.70
), (
5.71
), it follows that:

\Psi_{B,f,h}([{\hat{H}}(M),{\hat{H}}(N)]S)=i\Psi_{B,f,h}(\widehat{\{H(M),H(N)\}}S),\;\;\;\forall S.

(5.72)

From (
5.72
) it follows that the commutator between a pair of Hamiltonian constraints is anomaly free on a domain consisting of the
finite linear span of off shell basis states.

6
Action of Spatial Diffeomorphisms

In section
6.1
we show that the group of semianalytic diffeomorphisms is represented without anomalies on any off shell basis state, and hence on the finite span of such states.
In section
6.2
we show that the continuum limit (dual) action, of the Hamiltonian constraint as well as that of the product of a pair of Hamiltonian constraints, on any off shell basis state
is diffeomorphism covariant. As we shall see, the key feature of our constructions which simply and directly ensures diffeomorphism covariance is the tying of the choice of regulating coordinates (i.e. the RNC’s) to the metric label
of the off shell basis state. In section
6.3
, we discuss the conditions under which the results of section
6.2
extend to the finite span of off shell basis states.

6.1
Action on
\Psi_{B,f,h}

Let the unitary operator corresponding to the semianalytic diffeomorphism
\phi
be
{\hat{U}}(\phi)
.
and denote the diffeomorphic image of the state
S
by
{\hat{U}}(\phi)
by
S_{\phi}
.
Let
S
have overlap in
B
.
From the discussion around equations (
3.28
), (
3.29
), it follows that

S_{\phi}
also has overlap in
B
.
Then we have that:

\displaystyle\Psi_{B,f,h}({\hat{U}}^{\dagger}(\phi)S)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Psi_{B,f,h}(S_{\phi^{-1}})

(6.1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g_{S_{\phi^{-1}},h}\gamma_{S_{\phi^{-1}}}\prod_{v\in V(S_{\phi^{-1}})}f(v).

(6.2)

Clearly every
v\in V(S_{\phi^{-1}})
is the image by
\phi^{-1}
of some
\bar{v}\in V(S)
so that:

\prod_{v\in V(S_{\phi^{-1}})}f(v)=\prod_{\bar{v}\in V(S)}f(\phi^{-1}(v))=\prod_{\bar{v}\in V(S)}(\phi^{*}f)(\bar{v})

(6.3)

where we use
Wald’s notation

[
24
]
in which
(\phi^{*}f)
denotes the
push forward
of
f
with respect to
\phi
.
Next in order to emphasize its dependence on the metric, let us denote the distance
d
, defined in section
3.3
with respect to the metric
h
, by
d_{h}
.
It is straightforward to verify that for any pair of points
p,q\in\Sigma
,

d_{h}(\phi^{-1}p,\phi^{-1}q)=d_{\phi^{*}h}(p,q).

(6.4)

Since
g_{h}
is determined by the network of interkink distances with respect to
h
, and since any kink at the position
k
in
S
is moved to the position
\phi^{-1}(k)
in
S_{\phi^{-1}}

equation (
6.4
) immediately implies that:

g_{S_{\phi^{-1}},h}=g_{S,\phi^{*}h}.

(6.5)

Finally, it is straightforward to see that the diffeomorphism invariance of the RS volume operator together with (ii),
6
, section
3.1
(in particular, equation (
3.29
)) ensures that:

\gamma_{S_{\phi^{-1}}}=\gamma_{S}.

(6.6)

Equations (
6.2
), (
6.3
), (
6.5
), (
6.6
) together with definition of off shell state amplitudes (
3.11
) imply that:

\Psi_{B,f,h}({\hat{U}}^{\dagger}(\phi)S)=g_{S,\phi^{*}h}\gamma_{S}\prod_{v\in V(S)}\phi^{*}f(v)=\Psi_{B,\phi^{*}f,\phi^{*}h}(S).

(6.7)

On the other hand if
S
has no overlap in
B
,
the discussion around equations (
3.28
), (
3.29
)
implies that
S_{\phi^{-1}}
cannot have overlap in
B
so that for such
S

\Psi_{B,f,h}({\hat{U}}^{\dagger}(\phi)S)=0=\Psi_{B,\phi^{*}f,\phi^{*}h}(S),

(6.8)

with the last equality following from the fact that
S
has no overlap in
B
.

21
21

21

Note that (ii),
6
,section
3.1
in conjunction with the invertibility of
\phi
implies that
\phi
induces a bijection on
B
.

Equations (
6.7
), (
6.8
) together imply that:

{\hat{U}}(\phi)\Psi_{B,f,h}=\Psi_{B,\phi^{*}f,\phi^{*}h}

(6.9)

where the equality is of two elements of the algebraic dual to the finite span of spin nets. It follows that:

\displaystyle{\hat{U}}(\phi_{2}){\hat{U}}(\phi_{1})\Psi_{B,f,h}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\hat{U}}(\phi_{2})\Psi_{B,\phi_{1}^{*}f,\phi_{1}^{*}h}

(6.10)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Psi_{B,\phi_{2}^{*}\phi_{1}^{*}f,\phi_{2}^{*}\phi_{1}^{*}h}=\Psi_{B,(\phi_{2}\circ\phi_{1})^{*}f,(\phi_{2}\circ\phi_{1})^{*}h}

(6.11)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\hat{U}}(\phi_{2}\circ\phi_{1})\Psi_{B,f,h}.

(6.12)

Equation (
6.12
) shows that the group of semianalytic diffeomorphisms is represented without anomaly on any basis off shell state.
It is immediate to see that our considerations above extend to the finite span of such basis states and that diffeomorphisms map elements of this finite span
into the finite span so that
the group of semianalytic diffeomorphisms is represented without anomaly on this finite span.

Finally, consider a pair of diffeomorphisms
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
such that
\phi_{1}\neq\phi_{2}
.
From (
6.9
), we have
{\hat{U}}(\phi_{i})\Psi_{B,f,h}=\Psi_{B,\phi_{i}^{*}f,\phi_{i}^{*}h},i=1,2
.
Since
h
has no symmetries it follows that
\phi_{1}^{*}h\neq\phi_{2}^{*}h
. Then the considerations of section
7

imply that
\Psi_{B,\phi_{1}^{*}f,\phi_{1}^{*}h}\neq\Psi_{B,\phi_{2}^{*}f,\phi_{2}^{*}h}
. Conversely, it follows that if

{\hat{U}}(\phi_{1})\Psi_{B,f,h}={\hat{U}}(\phi_{2})\Psi_{B,f,h}
, it must be the case that
\phi_{1}=\phi_{2}
.
This implies that the representation of diffeomorphisms on the finite span of basis off shell states is also
faithful
.

6.2
Diffeomorphism Covariance on an off shell basis state

Diffeomorphism covariance of the single Hamiltonian constraint action is the statement that:

{\hat{U}}^{\dagger}(\phi){\hat{H}}(N){\hat{U}}(\phi)=H(\phi_{*}N),\forall\phi

(6.13)

where
\phi
is a semianalytic
C^{r}
diffeomorphism. The dual action amplitude of the left hand side operator on an off shell basis state
\Psi_{B,f,h}
is

{\hat{U}}^{\dagger}(\phi){\hat{H}}(N){\hat{U}}(\phi)\Psi_{B,f,h}(S)
. We have that:

\displaystyle{\hat{U}}^{\dagger}(\phi){\hat{H}}(N){\hat{U}}(\phi)\Psi_{B,f,h}(S)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\hat{U}}^{\dagger}(\phi){\hat{H}}(N)\Psi_{B,\phi^{*}f,\phi^{*}h}(S)

(6.14)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\Psi_{B,\phi^{*}f,\phi^{*}h}({\hat{H}}(N){\hat{U}}(\phi)S)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\Psi_{B,\phi^{*}f,\phi^{*}h}({\hat{H}}_{\epsilon}(N){\hat{U}}(\phi)S).

Here we have used (
6.9
) in the first line. In the second line we have used the definition of dual action. In doing so we have used the flexibility, given its classical reality, of calling
the Hamiltonian constraint in the first line
{\hat{H}}(N)
instead of
{\hat{H}}^{\dagger}(N)
. In the third line we have used the definition of the continuum limit action. Hence the
diffeomorphism covariance condition can be written in terms of amplitudes as:

\Psi_{B,\phi^{*}f,\phi^{*}h}({\hat{H}}(N){\hat{U}}(\phi)S)=\Psi_{B,f,h}(H(\phi_{*}N)S)\;\;\;\forall\;\;S.

(6.15)

From section
4.2
both the left hand side and the right hand side of the above equation are known. Below, we show their equality.
As noted above, if
S
has no overlap in
B
, neither does
{\hat{U}}(\phi)S
and (
4.38
) implies that

\Psi_{B,\phi^{*}f,\phi^{*}h}({\hat{H}}(N){\hat{U}}(\phi)S)=\Psi_{B,f,h}(H(\phi_{*}N)S)=0\;\;\;\forall\;\;S\;\;{\rm with}\;{\rm no}\;{\rm overlap}\;{\rm in}\;B.

(6.16)

On the other hand,
as noted above (see the discussion around (
3.28
), (
3.29
))
if
S
has overlap in
B
so does
{\hat{U}}(\phi)S
and we may use (
4.39
) to evaluate both sides of (
6.15
).
Below, we denote
{\hat{U}}(\phi)S
by
S_{\phi}
for notational convenience. In what follows, it is important to keep track of the metric dependence of various quantities. Accordingly,
we restore the metric subscript to the interkink distance function so as to denote the interkink distance function
for the state
S
with respect to metric
h
by
g_{S,h}
.

We also append a subscript
h
to the unit edge tangent
{\hat{e}}_{I_{v}}^{a}
with respect to
h
so as to denote it by
{\hat{e}}_{I_{v},h}^{a}
.
We also denote the RNCs
\{x\}
with respect to the metric
h
by
\{x\}_{h}
and the lapse computed at point
v
with respect to the RNCs
\{x\}_{h}
centered at
v
by
N(x_{h}(v))
.
Then for any
S
with overlap in
B
, equation F (
4.39
) implies that
the left hand side of (
6.15
) is:

\displaystyle\Psi_{B,\phi^{*}f,\phi^{*}h}({\hat{H}}(N){\hat{U}}(\phi)S)

(6.17)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{8\pi}\sum_{v\in V_{GR}(S_{\phi}))}N(x_{\phi^{*}h}(v))g_{S_{\phi},\phi^{*}h}\gamma_{(S_{\phi})_{\lambda_{v}}}(\prod_{v^{\prime}\neq v}(\phi^{*}f)(v^{\prime}))(\sum_{I_{v}=1}^{N_{v}}\frac{1}{4}{\hat{e}}_{I_{v},\phi^{*}h}^{a}\partial_{a}(\phi^{*}f)(v))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{8\pi}\sum_{\bar{v}\in V_{GR}(S))}N(x_{\phi^{*}h}(\phi(\bar{v})))g_{S_{\phi},\phi^{*}h}\gamma_{(S_{\phi})_{\lambda_{\phi(\bar{v})}}}(\prod_{\bar{v}^{\prime}\neq\bar{v}}(\phi^{*}f)(\phi(\bar{v}^{\prime})))(\sum_{I_{\phi(\bar{v})}=1}^{N_{\phi(\bar{v})}}\frac{1}{4}{\hat{e}}_{I_{\phi(\bar{v})},\phi^{*}h}^{a}\partial_{a}(\phi^{*}f)(\phi(\bar{v})).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(6.18)

where we have used the invariance under diffeomorphisms of the nondegeneracy of vertices to replace
V_{GR}(S_{\phi})
by
V_{GR}(S)
in the third line and where
v^{\prime},{\bar{v}}^{\prime}
range over
V(S_{\phi}),V(S)
respectively (recall that
V_{GR}(S)
denotes the non-degenerate, (and in the case of overlap in
B
, exclusively) GR vertices of
S
whereas
V(S)
refers to all (and in the case of overlap in
B
, exclusively GR,) vertices of
S
).

Diffeomorphism covariance of the RS Volume operator implies that
(S_{\phi})_{\lambda_{\phi(\bar{v})}}
is the image of
S_{\lambda_{\bar{v}}}
by
\phi
, so that:

\sum_{I_{\phi(\bar{v})}=1}^{N_{\phi(\bar{v})}}\frac{1}{4}{\hat{e}}_{I_{\phi(\bar{v})},\phi^{*}h}^{a}\partial_{a}(\phi^{*}f)(\phi(\bar{v}))=\sum_{I_{\bar{v}}=1}^{N_{\bar{v}}}\frac{1}{4}{\hat{e}}_{I_{\bar{v}},h}^{a}\partial_{a}f(\bar{v}).

(6.19)

It also follows that the elements of the kink set of
S_{\phi}
are diffeomorphic images by
\phi
of elements of the kink set of
S
. It immediately follows from the definition of the interkink distance function
g_{S,k}

in section
3.4.1
that:

g_{S_{\phi},\phi^{*}h}=g_{S,h}.

Next we turn to a proof of diffeomorphism invariance of the product of two Hamiltonian constraints. Since the proof is along the same lines as for the single Hamiltonian constraint action, we shall be brief. We need to show that:

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\left(\lim_{{\bar{\epsilon}}\rightarrow 0}\Psi_{B,\phi^{*}f,\phi^{*}h}({\hat{H}}_{\bar{\epsilon}}(M){\hat{H}}_{\epsilon}(N){\hat{U}}(\phi)S)\right).
